A $25 million MEV ruling exposes a dangerous blind spot in Wall Street’s tokenization push

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

An Ethereum exploit case has brought renewed attention to transaction-ordering risks as the SEC allows tokenized stock venues to set their own safeguards.

A federal judge on Nov. 16 acquitted Anton and James Peraire-Bueno in a $25 million Ethereum maximum extractable value (MEV) exploit case because prosecutors had failed to prove a material misrepresentation.

The decision leaves a separate question for tokenized US stock markets about what protects investors when transaction ordering works against them.

The SEC had already opened a conditional route for those markets in September on its framework, which preserves applicable securities antifraud and manipulation laws, but requires venues to disclose their policies for MEV even when they have none.

MEV concerns the value captured through transaction ordering. Four operators' notices show how much can differ within that framework, from transaction privacy and planned sequencing controls to the prospect that investors bear their own losses.

In her 46-page opinion, US District Judge Jessica Clarke found insufficient evidence to sustain wire fraud and the related wire-fraud and money-laundering conspiracy charges. The missing element was a material misrepresentation in the brothers' Omakase operation.

The opinion describes a 2023 operation that used lure transactions to attract sandwich-trading bots and exploited a relay-code vulnerability, producing about $25 million in 12 seconds. The targeted bots themselves sought to profit by placing transactions around someone else's trade, a practice that can leave that trader with a worse execution price.

The case was more specific than a simple claim that someone profited from transaction ordering, since prosecutors had to establish the charged fraud.

Clarke found that the trial evidence did not establish a material misrepresentation in the Omakase strategy, and frustrating MEV-Boost's intended design could not supply that missing element.

The court also distinguished precedents involving spoofing, where orders create a misleading impression before being canceled. Here, the lure transactions executed and also were not placed on a regulated market with rules prohibiting the conduct relied upon in those precedents.

That reasoning does not establish a universal requirement that wire fraud occur on a regulated market, nor does it determine whether all forms of MEV extraction are lawful.

In a March 30 letter, the Securities Industry and Financial Markets Association (SIFMA) argued that exposing pending trades to sandwiching and front-running can conflict with securities-market expectations. It called for technical protections or fair-sequencing rules when automated market makers handle securities.

An automated market maker (AMM) lets participants trade against assets committed to a pool using software-defined pricing. The ordering of those trades can affect who receives a favorable price and who captures the resulting profit.

Keeping an investor's pending instruction private and deciding who processes it first are economically significant choices.

The SEC's Sept. 17 order created temporary, conditional relief from the definition of an exchange for qualifying tokenized National Market System stock venues using permissioned AMM pools. Certain liquidity providers trading proprietary capital received separate relief from the dealer definition until Sept. 17, 2031.

The order retains applicable securities antifraud and antimanipulation provisions, including Exchange Act Section 10(b) and Rule 10b-5. Its conditions include permissioned access, public notices, records, and specified trading stoppages.

A venue must describe policies and procedures designed to address it, or state that it has none. It must disclose known material risks, explain any mitigation and compensation arrangements, and describe trading-abuse monitoring or acknowledge its absence.

A venue's published account of its protections can be detailed while leaving investors exposed to risks the operator expressly acknowledges.

The SEC's public list, reviewed Nov. 16, contains four notice filers. Their submission dates range from TSV LLC's Sept. 23 notice to Formation Securities' Nov. 9 filing.

TSV LLC's current notice says Canton's architecture generally avoids broadcasting readable pending transaction contents to unrelated participants, aiming to reduce opportunities for conventional public-mempool front-running and sandwich attacks.

The operator still acknowledges risks of information leakage, sequencing, compromised infrastructure, and collusion.

OKXICE's Nov. 5 notice disclosed that it has no policies and procedures designed to address MEV. That sits alongside general protections, including permissioned participation, participant minimum-output settings, surveillance and the ability to revoke access for manipulative trading. OKXICE says those features mitigate, rather than eliminate, MEV risk.

Its planned pools use Uniswap v4 on XLayer, where the notice says the sequencer generally orders transactions according to offered network fees.

Perpetuals.com's notice proposes generally first-in, first-out sequencing, with priority for trading-halt instructions and signed arrival receipts intended to make ordering verifiable. Yet its affiliated sequencer would receive nonpublic transaction information.

A private pending-transaction route can reduce exposure to outsiders while leaving a concentrated information advantage to manage.

Its October notice describes safeguards that were not fully implemented and surveillance intended to precede trading. Formation's notice likewise describes planned safeguards for its Solana venue.

Preventing an abusive trade, detecting it afterward, and compensating its victim are different functions.

TSV says participants generally should not expect compensation for market or impermanent losses, compromised participant systems or losses caused by unaffiliated third parties, unless it expressly agrees otherwise or the law requires it.

Perpetuals.com reports no established compensation policy for the risks described in its notice. Formation specifically excludes compensation for lost or compromised private keys or wallets, which does not establish a general recovery guarantee for other losses.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Criminal proof, securities-venue duties and compensation terms address different parts of investor protection.

A disclosed safeguard is not a guarantee of prevention or repayment.

The acquittal concerns this prosecution. Venue notices include plans and operator statements, not independently tested controls. Compensation depends on the particular loss, applicable law and agreements; the graphic predicts no liability outcome.

Qualitative mapping of the court's material-misrepresentation finding, the SEC's MEV disclosure and preserved-law provisions, and disclosed venue compensation limits. Branches show distinct questions, not sequence or money flows.

The acquittal concerns this prosecution. Venue notices include plans and operator statements, not independently tested controls. Compensation depends on the particular loss, applicable law and agreements; the graphic predicts no liability outcome.

The SEC order recognizes that sequencing can support price-stabilizing activity as well as abuse. TSV says it does not intend to prevent lawful ordinary post-execution arbitrage or price alignment that avoids misuse of confidential information and other prohibited conduct.

The dividing line cannot simply be whether someone profits after another trade. Relevant distinctions include whether pending information was misused, how the venue controls ordering, what conduct it addresses, and which legal obligations apply.

For institutions considering these markets, the notices expose concrete choices about privacy, sequencing, surveillance and loss allocation. The execution model determines that a private route still needs controls over privileged information, while a public route needs a response to abusive ordering.

The next disputed on-chain securities trade would raise questions about what representations were made, what rules applied, who controlled the information and ordering, and what recourse the investor had. Bringing stocks onto a blockchain makes those choices visible.

US judge clears brothers in $25M Ethereum MEV case after mistrial

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Judge Jessica Clarke said the transactions did not involve false promises, but the ruling did not determine whether the brothers’ conduct was ethical or violated other laws.

A US federal judge acquitted two brothers accused of extracting $25 million from Ethereum trading bots, ruling that prosecutors failed to prove they made material misrepresentations during the alleged exploit.

In a Monday ruling, Judge Jessica Clarke acquitted Anton and James Peraire-Bueno on wire fraud and related charges. Prosecutors alleged that the MIT-educated brothers exploited a flaw in maximal extractable value (MEV)-boost software in April 2023 to lure sandwich-trading bots into transactions they then disrupted, securing $25 million in about 12 seconds.

The judge said the transactions did not amount to false promises or constitute criminal fraud, and that the case was not about whether the brothers’ actions were ethical or potentially violated other laws.

Their first trial ended in a mistrial when jurors failed to reach a verdict in November 2025.

Coin Center executive director Peter Van Valkenburgh called the acquittal “excellent news,” saying prosecutors’ honest validator theory risked allowing the government to impose its own standards of conduct on Ethereum validators through wire fraud laws.

“Anyone participating in an open network would have to wonder whether prosecutors might later declare their behavior ‘dishonest’ and haul them into court,” Valkenburgh said.

The ruling also cited a 2025 decision involving Mango Markets trader Avraham Eisenberg, where a judge found insufficient evidence of false representations.

My Former Hedge Fund Portfolio Manager Just Shorted Berkshire Hathaway and SpaceX. Is It Time to Sell the Stocks?

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

My former boss is betting against two popular stocks.

Before becoming a contributing Motley Fool stock market analyst, I worked as a senior equity analyst for a long-short hedge fund for several years. While the fund has since closed, my former portfolio manager, Bill Martin, now runs his own investments and has made a name for himself by correctly predicting the collapse of Silicon Valley Bank in 2023. However, I was surprised to see on the social media platform X that he has decided to short both Berkshire Hathaway (BRKB -0.01%) (BRKA -0.25%) and Space Exploration Technologies (SPCX -1.26%).

Berkshire, of course, is the insurance conglomerate that was previously run by famed investor Warren Buffett, who recently stepped down from the company at the start of this year. Buffett is widely regarded as one of the world's best investors and has grown Berkshire into one of the world's largest companies.

Through his Raging Capital Ventures X account, Martin wrote that he recently bought Berkshire Hathaway B-share March $550 puts for a little over $10 to short the stock. He noted that the out-of-the-money puts were a cheap way to get short a large amount of notional exposure to the name (in other words, he's putting up a relatively small amount of money to take a large short position).

His short thesis is pretty simple: He thinks the stock's valuation looks stretched after a nice rally, while the conglomerate's insurance business is showing signs that it is starting to weaken. He also has not been impressed with Buffett's successor, Greg Abel.

While my former portfolio manager has a strong track record in shorting stocks, I would never recommend it to individual investors, given the risks and the fact that, over time, good stocks tend to move up.

Berkshire's stock has shot up, and its price-to-book (P/B) ratio has risen from around 1.4 earlier this year to 1.6. That's a bit high in my view, but not inconsistent with where the stock has traded in the past three years. Berkshire is often viewed as a market safe haven, especially now, given its still-large cash hoard. As such, while I wouldn't be a buyer at current levels, I think long-term investors can feel comfortable holding the stock.

At the same time that he discussed his Berkshire short, Martin also revealed that he was short SpaceX. His short thesis was quite simple. One of the biggest reasons he is bearish on the stock is that he believes SpaceX is already over-owned. He joked that "everyone and their brother" owns the stock, including endowment funds and venture capital firms. The argument here is that without incremental buyers, it becomes difficult for a stock to climb higher.

At the same time, he pointed out how capital-intensive SpaceX's growth initiatives are and how most are aimed at already competitive markets. He also noted how the midterm elections were a big setback for CEO Elon Musk, who poured money into largely losing Republican candidates. This likely won't provide SpaceX with any needed political capital as it pursues ventures that will require government approval.

That last point is particularly poignant, as SpaceX relies heavily on government approvals, environmental and land permits, and federal agency sign-offs. The company's largest growth engine is terrestrial data centers, where the permitting environment is expected to become much tougher. Meanwhile, SpaceX would need FCC approvals to build out a U.S. mobile network, and it needs FAA launch licenses for its space business.

Interestingly, though, an overall slower AI build-out may be good for SpaceX. The company is getting premium pricing for its short-term AI infrastructure rentals, giving it quick payback periods. If overall industry compute capacity grows more slowly than previously expected, this could keep short-term GPU rental prices high to the company's benefit.

While I think SpaceX faces a number of risks that could derail the stock, I think betting against Musk and the company at this stage is also pretty risky.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

How to stop your floorboards creaking this winter — 5 top tips from an expert

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Winter is a time when we spend more time indoors. It's also a time when you might start to notice some noises in your home that you may not have heard in the warmer months. And there's one noise in particular that's started to bother me – the squeaking and creaking of floorboards.

I'd wrongly assumed that I was noticing it more because my little kids are growing up and making noticeably less noise (or arguably, at times, more). But certainly at night I hear it – and it turns out there's a reason.

Here's why your floorboards are creaking and an expert's top tips on how to stop it.

Tony Reynolds, Managing Director at Supreme Merchants has worked in the builders' merchant industry for 15 years. So, he's pretty clued up on the workings of a home.

And for creaking floorboards in the colder months of the year, he reveals: "During winter, when the heating is on more frequently, indoor air can become much drier, causing wooden floorboards to lose some of their moisture."

He adds: "As the timber dries, it can shrink slightly, creating small gaps between boards or around the fixings. When you walk across the floor, the boards can then rub against each other or the joists underneath, which is what causes that familiar creaking sound."

Fortunately, you don't have to put up with the creaking floorboards in exchange for a warm home as Reynolds has shared his 5 top tips for silencing the woodwork, for good.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

When the cold weather hits, it can be tempting to turn your heating up to max, hoping to shock it into warming up your home. Reynolds says don't: "Turning the heating up dramatically can cause indoor conditions to fluctuate." Instead, he suggests doing it gradually so the timber can get used to it.

While loose boards and gaps can be there all year round, the change in temperature will be sure to highlight them. So, if you notice certain spots keep creaking, you may need to stabilize and secure the floorboards.

If it's a gap, Reynolds warns: "Avoid filling them with unsuitable materials, as the wood may expand again when humidity rises."

As mentioned, dry indoor air can be the cause of creaking floorboards. But lowering your heating levels isn't always necessary to combat it. Instead, Reynolds explains: "Maintaining a more consistent humidity level can help reduce excessive moisture changes in timber."

For this, he recommends buying a humidifier if you've noticed the air in your home is particularly dry.

This Levoit humidifier has a 4.2 liter capacity for big rooms, an easy top-fill, night light, aroma diffuser and can run for 42 hours. It's even easy to clean with a specialized detachable tray meaning you can empty and wipe it ready to move around your home where you're noticing dry air.

It's not just the floorboards themselves that can be loose; sometimes it's the nails and screws that are holding them in place. If you've noticed a particularly creaky and loose spot, Reynolds adds: "Appropriate flooring fixings can help reduce this movement."

Finally, and hopefully unlikely, is keeping an eye out for any damp patches around your home. "If floorboards feel soft, appear discolored, or have developed a musty smell, investigate further," Reynolds explains. These can be signs of a more serious issue that you don't want to ignore.

So, instead of just hopping over the creaking floorboards and hoping they'll go away, it's better to address the issue as soon as possible.

Fortunately, with these top tips, you'll be able to keep seasonal creaking to a minimum. "The important thing is to understand why the boards are moving rather than simply trying to silence the noise."

PostgreSQL patches 52 security holes, sends version 14 into retirement

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Some fixes need more than installation, while the next major release is due November 19

PostgreSQL developers have patched 52 security vulnerabilities and more than 140 bugs in updates spanning versions 14 to 18, alongside a second release candidate for version 19.

The batch includes PostgreSQL 14.25, the final release for the now-unsupported version 14. Users are advised to move to a newer major version.

Some fixes also require follow-up work, including rebuilding affected indexes or taking a fresh full backup. Administrators should check the release's post-update instructions.

Alastair Turner, community technologist at open source consultancy Percona, said the high-severity code execution flaws should be taken seriously, although exploiting them requires database access.

"This isn't something just a brute-force scan of the right port is going to find," he said. "They can make compromises through SQL injection or credential theft a lot worse, but don't create the opportunity for remote, anonymous code execution."

Among the fixes is CVE-2026-6460, a type confusion flaw that could let a database user with permission to create objects execute arbitrary code. It carries a CVSS severity score of 8.8 out of 10.

Turner attributed the growing number of security discoveries partly to AI-assisted scanning and partly to increased scrutiny as PostgreSQL gains ground in enterprise computing.

"It is a record number of CVEs fixed in a quarterly release, and I don't think we've seen the end of that trend. Looking down the list of where these CVEs came from, we see Anthropic and OpenAI open source scanners, so it is clear AI or brute force is playing a role," he said.

Turner said the fixes spanned multiple parts of the software. There "isn't one piece of code that was broken" but rather "a wide base that got patched."

"It's also worth noting that this is the third quarterly release in a row which sets a new record for the number of CVEs patched," he added.

PostgreSQL 14 users now face a major-version upgrade or running without further security and bug fixes. Their destination may depend on internal upgrade policies. Organizations that require the previous major release, known as N-1, would currently target PostgreSQL 17, Turner noted. Version 18 remains the latest generally available release until 19 arrives.

PostgreSQL 19 is scheduled for general availability on Thursday, November 19, barring further problems. It is set to include a new REPACK command, whose CONCURRENTLY option allows reads and writes to continue while disk space is reclaimed. VACUUM FULL requires an exclusive table lock, blocking reads and writes while it runs. ®

Crimea bridge shut after Ukrainian strike, as Russia hits Kyiv crossings

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Images circulated on social media appeared to show part of the road section of the Crimea bridge has collapsed

A key bridge linking occupied Crimea and Russia is closed after a Ukrainian strike inflicted major damage on the prized Kremlin asset.

Images shared by Ukrainian officials showed a section of the Crimean Bridge - also known as the Kerch Bridge - had collapsed into the water.

Russian officials confirmed the structure had been damaged by drones, while Russia's deputy prime minister was due to chair an emergency meeting to discuss the situation.

Meanwhile, Kyiv Mayor Vitali Klitschko said the Northern and Southern bridges, both important crossings over the Dnipro River, were closed after Russia targeted the Ukrainian capital overnight.

Former Ukrainian adviser Anton Gerashchenko shared images and video footage appearing to show extensive damage to the road section of the Crimea bridge, with remnants of the support arch and road section seen protruding from the water below.

The bridge, which has come under repeated attack by Ukrainian forces, was built by Russia after it invaded and annexed the peninsula in 2014, and was personally opened by Russian President Vladimir Putin in 2018.

While Ukraine has been trying to increase the cost to Russia of waging war economically, by hitting oil refineries and depots, striking the Crimea bridge is an attempt to increase the political cost on Putin.

The timing of the Crimea bridge strike comes at a significant moment, ahead of Putin meeting the leaders of China and the US. This strike could cloud his moment back in the spotlight.

While the long-term impact of the strike on was not immediately clear, the bridge has been used to move troops and supplies into Crimea and towards the front line.

The railway section of the bridge appears to still be intact but it has been damaged previously.

Russia's Investigative Committee confirmed structural damage, and said it had opened a "criminal case... on terrorism charges" after attacks by "Ukraine's armed formations".

The Crimea bridge (pictured in 2023) has been repeatedly targeted by Ukraine - one of the two distinctive white arches appears to have collapsed overnight

Monday night's strikes came a day after Ukraine began its most recent attempt to disable the Crimea bridge, in an attack which a Russian official said killed two and reportedly caused less significant structural damage.

Crimea's Moscow-backed governor Sergei Aksyonov confirmed on Telegram that traffic over the bridge had been suspended.

Zelensky had warned of major attacks in the days leading up to a meeting between Putin, Donald Trump and Xi Jinping in China's Shenzhen on Thursday.

Meanhile, loud explosions were heard in Kyiv just before midnight on Tuesday, with Klitschko saying shortly after that the city had suffered "damage to critical infrastructure systems" and disruption to heating and water.

The Northern Bridge (pictured in October) is closed after being targeted again by Russia

Some of Kyiv's above-ground subway services were damaged in the overnight attacks, officials said, after civilians were told to go to civil defence shelters.

Residents of Kyiv typically shelter in the city's underground stations during air raids.

Eight major bridges span the Dnipro River connecting the western and eastern parts of Kyiv, and an estimated three million residents rely on the bridges to travel across the city.

These latest Russian strikes will cause major disruption, with traffic already reportedly building in the capital.

Elsewhere, three people have been injured in a Russian missile attack in the Cherkast region, according to local officials.

The latest round of tit-for-tat strikes came after US, Ukrainian and European officials held talks on ways to facilitate "direct engagement" between Russia and Europe to end the war.

Following Monday's meeting, US envoy Jared Kushner, President Trump's son-in-law, said the US was committed to providing Ukraine with a "strong, credible security guarantees", as well as a path towards EU membership.

He added that Nato "will remain a critical pillar of European security", and that preparations are underway for a further multilateral meeting "in the near future".

Zelensky said that the meeting was "difficult", adding that more needed to be done to support Ukraine's defence against Russian ballistic missile strikes, which he said were being provided by North Korea.

The Ukrainian president has long called for European nations to be included in any peace talks, which he argues is necessary because of Kyiv's longer-term ambition to join the EU.

Meanwhile, Ukrainian officials said the final death toll from a Russian attack on the southern Ukrainian village of Kamyshuvakha earlier this month had reached 72.

Four people are still considered missing after the bombing, which Ukrainian officials said was one of the deadliest individual attacks since the start of the war in 2022.

Footage, from a camera on one of the killed police officers, showed residents going to an underground shelter after the initial attack on 2 November. Soon after a blast is heard and flames are seen as the shelter is hit.

Ukrainian police said of those killed were police officers, "staff of the gymnasium, utility workers and medical personnel".

Russia's launched its full-scale invasion of Ukraine in February 2022. Approximately one-fifth of Ukraine is now under Russian occupation and Kyiv has refused Putin's demand to give up areas of Ukraine's eastern Donbas that remain under its control.

Judge Acquits MIT Brothers in $25M Ethereum MEV Case, Ending Prosecution

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Judge Acquits MIT Brothers in $25M Ethereum MEV Case, Ending Prosecution

A federal judge in Manhattan has acquitted Anton and James Peraire-Bueno, the MIT-educated brothers accused of exploiting Ethereum's transaction ordering system to take $25 million from automated trading bots.

U.S. District Judge Jessica Clarke granted the brothers' motion for a judgment of acquittal on Monday, clearing them of wire fraud, conspiracy to commit wire fraud and conspiracy to commit money laundering, according to her ruling. The decision closes the criminal case without a second trial.

While the brothers' scheme "may have frustrated the spirit" of MEV-Boost, software that lets Ethereum validators outsource the ordering of transactions to specialized block builders, that was not enough to make out fraud, Clarke said. In a footnote, she added that the prosecution "raises serious due process concerns," though she declined to rule on that question.

Because the money laundering charge relied on the proceeds of the alleged wire fraud, it fell with the fraud counts.

The case stemmed from an April 2023 exploit targeting so-called sandwich bots, automated traders that profit from maximal extractable value (MEV) by placing their own trades on either side of other users' transactions.

According to the ruling, the brothers placed bait transactions designed to lure specific bots, then exploited a bug in a relay's code to see the bots' bundled trades before committing to a block. They then swapped the bait transactions for trades that sold the tokens instead, leaving the bots with about $25 million in losses in roughly 12 seconds. Authorities arrested the pair in May 2024, saying they spent months planning the attack and took steps to hide the proceeds.

At trial in Manhattan last year, an insider testified that the brothers had allegedly planned the attack, and the defense had earlier sought to block evidence of their Google searches. Clarke declared a mistrial in November 2025, after the jury deadlocked.

The case was closely watched in the crypto industry. Clarke's ruling described it as a "first of its kind" prosecution, a label the government itself used in its indictment.

OpenAI bosses shrug off Anthropic’s IPO

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Finance chief Sarah Friar said OpenAI’s latest $1.25tn round was unplanned, sparked by an inaccurate news report.

OpenAI chief executive Sam Altman at The Grove, a Village Global event, in November 2022.

OpenAI’s top two executives said on Monday they are not worried about rival Anthropic going public first. Chief executive Sam Altman and finance chief Sarah Friar both spoke at Semafor’s Silicon Valley & The World event, as Brent Griffiths reported for Business Insider.

Asked whether OpenAI could end up like Lyft, which listed before Uber in 2019 but later fell far behind it, Altman brushed off the idea.

Anthropic made its S-1 filing public on 26 October and plans to list on Nasdaq under the ticker ANT. It is expected to debut this week.

Friar went further. Anthropic’s listing is good for the industry and good for OpenAI, she said, according to Semafor’s report. She added that OpenAI did not mind being second.

Friar also said OpenAI is closing a smaller funding round at a $1.25tn valuation, after raising $122bn in March. She described the round as unplanned and opportunistic.

It began, she said, after a well-known publication wrongly reported that OpenAI was raising money. Investors then contacted the company, Semafor reported.

Altman said journalists were more sceptical about AI than investors, who he described as bullish. He added that it was a good thing more investors wanted exposure to the AI boom.

He repeated that OpenAI will go public at some point, but warned that listing comes with costs.

“I think there are real downsides to being public,” Altman said, pointing to a volatile period for the industry and big decisions on safety.

OpenAI is still aiming for a 2027 listing. Friar told staff in August it would be public in 2027, or sooner if growth held.

We Now Know What Robert Pattinson Was Doing When the James Bond Casting News Broke

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

All thanks to filmmaker Robert Eggers, who directed the star in 2019's 'The Lighthouse.'

It’s been a whirlwind 48 hours for Robert Pattinson.

The Hollywood Reporter heard from sources that the British star only found out he’d been tapped to succeed Daniel Craig as James Bond on Sunday morning, when Denis Villeneuve — who’s helming the next Bond movie — called him. Producer Amy Pascal was said to have informed the other candidates.

Within a day, the whole world knew too. Amazon MGM Studios, which bought the creative rights to the Bond franchise from producer siblings Michael G. Wilson and Barbara Broccoli last year, released the news via the character’s official social media accounts. Just before 7 a.m. PT on Monday, a tuxedo-wearing Pattinson emerged from the shadows, gun in hand. His announcement post has accrued over 1.2 million likes on X at the time of writing. Related Stories News Over 400 U.K. Industry Figures Demand End to "Culture of Open Secrets" After Russell Brand Trial Movies Tallinn Market Debates Estonia's Film Sector and How Foreign Shoots "Bring Both Value and Tension"

Thanks to Robert Eggers, who directed Pattinson in his 2019 period horror-fantasy The Lighthouse, we now know what the next Bond was up to when that news became public and sent the internet into a tailspin. During an on-camera interview with IGN, Eggers was asked if, while Pattinson’s character killed a seagull during the making of The Lighthouse, he ever stopped to consider: “There goes the next James Bond.”

“No, but I’m not surprised that it panned out the way that it did,” replied the Nosferatu filmmaker. “And also, crazily enough, he was watching a screening of Werwulf when the news broke, and I was just texting with him about it this morning,” he said, laughing.

Robert Pattinson is James Bond pic.twitter.com/3GulkhblDp

Werwulf, set for release on Dec. 25 in the U.S., stars Eggers regulars Aaron Taylor-Johnson, Lily-Rose Depp, Willem Dafoe and Ralph Ineson, and is set to chronicle a cursed farmer’s transformation into a monstrous creature in 13th-century England. Coincidentally, Taylor-Johnson was among the rumored Bond contenders for a long time.

So what did our new Bond think of Werwulf? “Oh, he loved it, of course,” added Eggers.

Pattinson, who this year alone has starred in The Drama, The Odyssey, Primetime and still has Dune 3 to come, said on Monday through an Amazon MGM Studios press release: “James Bond is one of the most legendary characters in cinema, and to be entrusted with 007 is the honor of a lifetime. I’m deeply grateful to Denis, Amy, David, Tanya and everyone at Amazon MGM Studios for their belief in me. To say this role is a dream come true is an understatement. I couldn’t be more excited about this partnership and the work ahead with this fantastic team.”

He won a months-long race for the role, beating fellow screen-testers Callum Turner, Josh O’Connor and Jack Lowden. Villeneuve, who recently worked with Pattinson on the final Dune outing, is directing a script from Peaky Blinders mastermind Steven Knight.

The 40-year-old actor is also already the face of DC Studio’s Batman. THR took a deep dive into why his casting is the smart but safe choice as the Bond franchise enters a new era. Fan reaction to the announcement is here — and some are suggesting Pattinson’s starring role in Twilight predicted his future Bond role.

In fact, Pattinson might have even manifested it. In 2012, during the height of his Twilight fame, the actor was asked in an interview if he could be playing 007 in 10 years’ time. “Yeah, definitely,” he replied, “but more like 20 years. […] I think there’d be nothing worse than, like, ‘Let’s get a fresh-faced Bond!’ That would be the worst idea in the world. After Daniel Craig, you have to have some baggage. It would be ridiculous to reinvent [him] as some young posh kid. Or, if [he] was, I’d have to get so tortured in the first few scenes. I’d have to do the first film with one arm or something.”

Pikmin Bloom Has Upset Players By Referring To Taiwan As China

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sam Woods is TheGamer’s Senior Reporter. He’s been in games journalism since 2020 and has undertaken a variety of roles in that time. These have included roles such as SEO Editor, Managing Editor at DualShockers, and Global Head of Gaming, and he's also been seen on the BBC. He was promoted to the Senior Reporter position in 2025 following a series of high-quality reports and exclusives for TheGamer.

Since 2020, Sam has written over 3,000 articles and specializes in telling the stories of the people behind the games. He’s interviewed the biggest game directors, some of the industry’s top talents, told the stories of how indie games came to be, and spoken to players about what really makes them tick. He’s also interviewed some of the world’s biggest musical artists to find out how the medium has inspired their work, and TV and Film actors about their roles in video game adaptations. He even hosted a PAX panel in 2021 and made a documentary in 2024 surrounding the creation of a Disney game.

Alongside this, Sam focuses on breaking news. He got the scoop on Perfect Dark’s cancellation, provided context on some AI drama, and revealed that Lady Dimitrescu’s actress would love to step back into the character’s giant shoes. These articles have been picked up by the biggest gaming sites, including IGN, GameSpot, and PC Gamer. He also loves to dive into the numbers behind the games.

Sam’s journey in games journalism has taken him all over the world. He’s covered Gamescom in Germany, Summer Game Fest in Los Angeles, and Tokyo Game Show in Japan, each on three occasions.

He’s been learning Japanese since 2024 with the intention of identifying and translating Japanese-language news and bringing it to English-language audiences first, something he’s done countless times.

Sam’s first review was 2020’s Battletoads reboot, and he’s since reviewed games in The Legend of Zelda, Super Mario, and Pokemon franchises.

Tensions between Taiwan and China are at an all-time high. While the Chinese government, led by Xi Jinping, believes Taiwan is part of China, Taiwan, its government, and its people view the country as an independent, sovereign state. Escalated military drills have taken place over the last year, as outside nations try to calm the issue.

Pikmin Bloom, the mobile Pokemon Go-like Pikmin title, has accidentally waded into the topic by labeling Taiwan as part of China in a recent update, leading to hurt feelings and boycott calls from people in the island nation.

First reported by Taiwanese outlet 4gamers, Pikmin Bloom, a game developed by Scopely, has referred to Taiwan as "Taiwan, China."

An announcement for the game's 5th Anniversary event used the label in the Simplified Chinese version of the release. While 4gamers confirmed that the Traditional Chinese announcement reads "stores throughout Taiwan," the Simplified Chinese version said, "Japan and the Taiwan region of China."

The issue was spotted by Sherry Chou on Threads, who, in their post [translated by Google Translate], said:

"Isn't this game completely unplayable in China? Taiwanese players are still the main source of income, so why are they still trying to curry favor with China? Are the official Pikmin game developers brainless or just used to kneeling? This game is making money off Taiwanese players. As a low-spending player, I can't stand it! If it's not fixed, I'm quitting." The post was dubbed "heartbreaking" by another Pikmin Bloom player.

As a low-spending player, I can't stand it! If it's not fixed, I'm quitting.

Taiwan is currently Pikmin Bloom's biggest market. Spending from the country surged 902 percent in the first half of this year, as the walking/exploration game made $35.7 million, almost as much as it made in all of 2025. $18.5 million of the $35.7 million came from players in Taiwan, with the country taking a 52 percent market share in the first half of the year.

Japan still eclipses Taiwan in lifetime spend, contributing 49 percent of all-time revenue, but Taiwan is closing the gap. This growth in Taiwan has seen collaborations with local Taiwanese brands, including CoCo Fresh Tea & Juice and Family Mart.

It's the second time Pikmin has been involved in controversy in the last few days, with Pikmin 4's Dandori Academy Switch 2 Edition review-bombed and labeled a "scam."

Former Leeds United goalkeeper Paul Robinson does not believe there is “any need” for Three Lions boss Thomas Tuchel to immediately replace Jordan Pickford with James Trafford.

Game time has been evenly split between Everton’s Pickford and Leeds’ Trafford across the last two international breaks, with the latter pushing to dislodge the former.

Nvidia Stock Fell Below $170. Here’s Why It Could Be a Screaming Buy.

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nvidia lost its CEO and its crown, but the sell-off could be a gift for patient investors.

The bigger they are, the harder they fall. That adage is playing out before investors' eyes with Nvidia (NVDA -4.12%).

Throughout most of the past year, Nvidia ranked as the world's largest company by market cap. However, the chip stock plunged more than 30% below its previous high, forfeiting its top spot to Apple (AAPL -0.98%). Nvidia's shares now trade below $170 for the first time in nearly eight months.

But buying Nvidia on the dip has proven a smart strategy in the past. The stock could be a screaming buy yet again.

It's important to understand that while Nvidia's stock has pulled back sharply, its underlying business is booming. In the second quarter of fiscal 2027, the company's revenue more than doubled year over year to $96.2 billion. Its earnings skyrocketed 128%.

Nvidia is scheduled to announce its third-quarter results after the market close on Tuesday, Nov. 17, 2026. There's no reason to anticipate a dramatic slowdown. Wall Street expects the company to post year-over-year revenue growth of roughly 91% in Q3, with earnings up 90%.

The demand for Nvidia's GPUs remains strong. Its Blackwell architecture has been a resounding success -- and led in every category on two industry AI benchmarks.

The multi-rack AI supercomputer platform Vera Rubin began shipping in August 2026. Nvidia expects Vera Rubin to have the fastest product ramp in the company's history.

Meanwhile, Nvidia's trailing 12-month price-to-earnings ratio of 20.6 is at its lowest level since early 2019. Its valuation is even more attractive with growth factored in. Nvidia's forward earnings multiple is 10.3. Its price-to-earnings-to-growth (PEG) ratio, which is based on analysts' five-year earnings growth projections, is a super-low 0.22.

You might have noticed that I haven't yet mentioned the reason why Nvidia's stock has plunged recently. However, there's no way to completely ignore the elephant in the room: Jensen Huang's abrupt departure as CEO following allegations of two affairs, one with a person accused of being a Chinese spy and another with an Nvidia employee.

Huang's unexpected exit certainly leaves a void at Nvidia. He co-founded the company in 1993. He has been the visionary who led Nvidia to jaw-dropping growth.

But I don't think that Huang's absence means that Nvidia is doomed. For one thing, he didn't single-handedly drive the company's product vision. Nvidia has plenty of highly experienced and capable leaders who will ensure its technology advances, including longtime CFO and interim CEO Colette Kress.

Don't rule out the possibility that Huang could return, either. The Financial Times reported that he could attempt a comeback as Nvidia's CEO and might even try to get the White House to lobby on his behalf.

Maybe Huang will become Nvidia's CEO again; maybe not. Either way, the current sell-off looks like a great buying opportunity for patient investors.

What Is Backpack Crypto? BP Explodes 30% After CFTC Approval

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

First, what is Backpack crypto? Why is it exploding? Backpack is suddenly becoming one of the market’s hottest stories after securing a major regulatory breakthrough in the United States. Its native BP token surged 30% in 24 hours as traders rushed to price in the exchange’s latest expansion. The Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) has effectively opened the door for Backpack to challenge established crypto derivatives platforms on American soil.

The approval gives Backpack the regulatory foundation to operate a US derivatives exchange and clearinghouse. Its ambitions extend beyond simply offering another place to trade Bitcoin and Ethereum. The company wants to combine leveraged crypto trading, collateral management, and global liquidity under one regulated platform.

The development also arrives as competition heats up over who can deliver the next generation of crypto trading infrastructure.

Founded by Armani Ferrante, Backpack started as a Solana-focused crypto wallet and exchange. It has since expanded into securities, tokenization, and derivatives. The company then reports more than $450 billion in cumulative trading volume and customers across over 150 countries.

Its latest breakthrough involves two separate approvals from the CFTC. The Designated Contract Market (DCM) registration allows Backpack to operate a regulated derivatives exchange.

Backpack is coming home to the USA. 🇺🇸We’ve received landmark CFTC approval to establish the first-ever derivatives exchange powered by one order book with unified global liquidity.Leveraged perpetuals. Crypto collateral. Cross-margin. 24/7 trading.Join the waitlist:…

Meanwhile, its Derivatives Clearing Organization (DCO) registration provides the infrastructure needed to manage collateral, margin, and settlement obligations. Together, these approvals establish the foundation for Backpack to offer leveraged crypto perpetual futures in the United States.

The proposed platform will support 24/7 trading, crypto and fiat collateral, cross-margining, and access to Backpack’s global order book.

That last feature could prove particularly important. Rather than isolating American traders inside a separate liquidity pool, Backpack wants to connect US customers with its international trading ecosystem.

BP responded immediately. The token climbed by more than 30% over 24 hours to $1.10, pushing its market cap higher as trading activity accelerated. The rally ties to growing expectations that Backpack could become a serious competitor in the American derivatives market.

The regulatory breakthrough also strengthens its position against offshore platforms that have dominated leveraged crypto trading for years.

Backpack’s approval exposes an interesting opportunity across the wider crypto market. Bitcoin remains the industry’s largest liquidity pool, yet its base layer was never designed for sophisticated smart contracts, high-speed decentralized trading or complex margin products.

That creates an infrastructure gap developers are racing to fill. Solana demonstrated how quickly decentralized applications can execute transactions. Bitcoin, meanwhile, offers a much deeper pool of established capital.

Bringing those strengths together could unlock an entirely different market for Bitcoin-denominated trading.

Projects exploring this direction, Bitcoin Hyper ($HYPER), which combines Bitcoin Layer 2 infrastructure with Solana Virtual Machine technology. Its architecture aims to let users bridge BTC into a faster environment supporting smart contracts and decentralized applications.

The potential goes beyond faster transfers. Bitcoin liquidity could eventually support decentralized spot markets, margin trading, and perpetual futures without forcing traders to abandon Bitcoin for another blockchain ecosystem.

Bitcoin Hyper has already attracted more than $33.2 million through its presale, exceeding Backpack’s previously disclosed $17 million Series A funding round. Its token currently sells for just $0.0137, while its staking program advertises rewards of up to 35% APY.

With its November 26 launch target approaching, the presale is entering its final stretch. The combination of Bitcoin liquidity and Solana-style execution is attracting attention as developers look for ways to bring more sophisticated financial applications to Bitcoin.

Backpack now has something many offshore competitors cannot easily replicate: a regulatory pathway into the American derivatives market. The company still needs to turn its approvals into actual trading activity, but the potential market is enormous.

American traders represent an important source of institutional and retail liquidity. Bringing them into a unified order book could strengthen Backpack’s competitive position while attracting market makers and professional trading firms.

BP’s 30% rally shows investors are already anticipating that expansion, and Backpack’s regulatory breakthrough could mark the beginning of a much larger shift in crypto trading.

The race is no longer just about building another exchange. It is about connecting deeper liquidity, faster execution, and increasingly sophisticated financial products. Bitcoin’s enormous capital base makes that opportunity particularly difficult to ignore.

Zelda Switch 2 restock: Live restock updates at Walmart, Target, and Best Buy (Nov. 17)

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If your current morning ritual involves violently hitting refresh on a dozen retail tabs, you already know the vibe: finding the Nintendo Switch 2: The Legend of Zelda 40th Anniversary Edition is turning into an Olympic sport.

Between automated bot armies scooping digital drops, surprise order cancellations at Walmart, and inventory vanishing in seconds on Amazon, this custom black-and-gold console feels like a myth. But while digital storefronts look completely barren, stock is actively circulating behind the scenes.

Before getting into the restock details, bookmark these retailer pages to check for surprise drops:

Nintendo Store — Out of stock right now, but you can add it to your wishlist to get pinged the second a restock hits.

Amazon — Dropping tiny batches of consoles randomly throughout the day.

Walmart — Stock exists but only for third-party listings selling at way over market price, so make sure your in-stock email alerts are turned on.

Target — Sold out online, but periodic drops have taken place in-store.

Best Buy — Sold out online, but select stores ran midnight openings on Nov. 5 with walk-in stock.

GameStop — Online orders are off; you will have to walk into a store to find one.

The feeding frenzy is being supercharged by Nintendo’s staggered release calendar. While the custom Zelda 40th Anniversary hardware dropped first on Oct. 29, the flagship game driving demand — The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time for Nintendo Switch 2 — arrived on Thursday, Nov. 5.

The remake rebuilds the 1998 classic from the ground up with modernized controls, a fully orchestrated soundtrack, reworked dungeon puzzles, and expanded Hyrule environments. Since the $520 console doesn’t include the game, everyone is rushing to lock down a system before launch day.

The package includes the special-edition Switch 2 system sporting a custom black-and-gold finish, themed Joy-Con 2 controllers, and a matching dock decorated with the gold Triforce emblem. It retains the platform's signature flexibility, letting you switch between TV, Tabletop, and Handheld modes whenever you want. The best tech deal this week The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

Inside the themed chassis, you get 256GB of built-in storage (expandable with microSD Express cards), a vivid display in handheld mode, faster load times, and support for up to 4K resolution on compatible TVs when the console sits in the dock.

The magnetic Joy-Con 2 controllers add mouse-style tracking in supported games, while the new GameChat hub lets you voice chat and share your screen directly as you play. It also plays your older Switch games and supports standard multiplayer options.

Landing limited-edition Nintendo hardware takes a little prep work, but these battle-tested habits will give you a real edge over the bots:

Save your checkout details everywhere — Log in to Target, Best Buy, Walmart, and the Nintendo Store to save your shipping address and default card. Stopping to type a card number or billing zip code during a live drop is how you lose an order.

Hands off the refresh button in Best Buy's queue — When an online drop goes live, Best Buy puts shoppers into a randomized waiting line to filter out scrapers. Touching the reload button resets your spot and boots you straight to the back.

Watch for Walmart+ early-access paywalls — Walmart frequently locks headline console drops behind paid Walmart+ subscriptions for the first 30 to 60 minutes. If you have a membership, keep that tab signed in; if not, wait for the general public window to unlock.

Dodge third-party sellers — Double-check the seller before buying. Marketplace scalpers frequently list units for jacked-up prices; make sure it says "Sold and shipped by [Retailer]" at the standard price.

Skip the web wars and head to physical stores — Online checkout bots grab website stock in under 15 seconds. Showing up in person is easily your most reliable path to securing one without paying scalper markups.

Stores like Walmart, Amazon, and Best Buy often hold onto popular gaming hardware to use as headline deals during Black Friday and Cyber Monday week.

With that said, Black Friday restocks are usually the hardest ones to catch. You will be fighting website crashes, gift shoppers, and automated bots all at once. If you can grab a console whenever stock appears, take it. Think of Black Friday as a backup plan, not your main bet.

Console hunting sucks right now, period. But between the surprise micro-drops and physical store restocks, you still have real shots at retail price — just be ready to move the second that stock goes live.

When she's not tracking deals, she's usually comparing products, digging through reviews, and figuring out what actually makes a purchase worth it.

Live Updates: Trump’s Whirlwind Asia Trip Starts in the Philippines

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

President Trump’s 48-hour trip will begin with a meeting with Southeast Asian leaders in Manila. He will later meet Xi Jinping of China and Vladimir Putin of Russia in Shenzhen, a Chinese economic hub.

President Trump on Tuesday began a whirlwind Asia trip in which he will meet Xi Jinping of China and other world leaders, with an unpredictable agenda that could include the future of American tariffs, nuclear arms control and U.S. restrictions on advanced artificial intelligence chips.

Mr. Trump landed in Manila, where he is scheduled to meet with President Ferdinand R. Marcos Jr. of the Philippines and attend a gathering of Southeast Asian leaders. After less than 24 hours there, he will head to the APEC economic summit in Shenzhen, China, where all eyes will be on his expected meeting with Mr. Xi and President Vladimir V. Putin of Russia.

The brief itinerary appears to reflect Mr. Trump’s preferences. He dislikes big summits where he often is not the sole subject of attention. And he enjoys meeting with leaders of powerful nations — especially Mr. Xi and Mr. Putin, two authoritarian leaders he often talks about in admiring terms.

Mr. Trump will be meeting Mr. Xi for the third time this year, and a three-way meeting between China, Russia and the United States would be the first of its kind. Each leader will arrive at the session, planned for Thursday, with a different agenda, and some analysts have compared it to Yalta, where the leaders of the United States, Britain and the Soviet Union divided up postwar Europe in 1945.

That analogy has limits: No one thinks these three leaders, powerful as they are, are there to redefine the borders of any continent. But their very presence at one table would underscore they have major sway over the shape of the next world order. And for Mr. Trump, who loves the symbolism of a dramatic photo op, that may be enough.

On Tuesday, Mr. Trump will catch the end of a dayslong meeting of the Association of Southeast Asian Nations.

He isn’t just any latecomer: He is the one who issued tariffs on countries across the region, even on allies — bolstering the feeling in Asia that the United States is not a reliable partner, but rather one that treasures its commercial interests over its security interests. Southeast Asian leaders will be looking for any signs that the Republican defeat in the midterm elections may have humbled Mr. Trump or made him reconsider his policies.

Mr. Trump will also meet with leaders from the so-called “Quad” — the United States, India, Japan and Australia. All three partners are smarting from high energy prices triggered by the Iran war, a conflict Mr. Trump never discussed with them before launching it in late February.

Before leaving for China on Wednesday, Mr. Trump will be hosted by Mr. Marcos, who has been contending with Chinese challenges to islands that both Beijing and Manila claim.

In Shenzhen — the heart of China’s robotics and A.I. industries — Mr. Trump will pursue détente with Mr. Xi. He needs to extend a soon-expiring trade truce that limited tariffs on Chinese goods entering the United States in return for a Chinese commitment not to cut off rare earths and magnets.

Then comes the main event: the meeting with Mr. Xi and Mr. Putin.

“We are going to be talking about denuclearization, a tough subject because it is very hard for any of us to say we’ll get rid of our nukes,’’ Mr. Trump told reporters before leaving Washington, referring to the agenda of the trilateral meeting. “We will talk about Iran a little,” he said, insisting the situation was in “very good shape.”

Mr. Trump also said he would talk about Ukraine, on which Mr. Putin’s aides are engaging in talks with the U.S. and Ukrainian officials, but also renewing demands that NATO pull back its troops to where they were before the alliance expanded toward Russia.

On nuclear weapons, Chinese officials have made clear they are not interested in talks until their arsenal is roughly the size of the American one. And on the Iran war, analysts say it seems unlikely that the three-way meeting would make any progress — at a moment when Russia is happy to see energy prices remain high, and China is eager to see them rapidly decline.

A counter to China: Last December, the United States launched an initiative called Pax Silica to unite partners around A.I. supply chains independent of China. In the Philippines, America’s oldest ally in Asia, the backlash has been fierce. Read more ›

Philippine leader: President Trump’s well-known affinity for autocratic leaders was on display the last time he met with President Ferdinand R. Marcos Jr. of the Philippines. Read more ›

President Trump has landed in Manila, lingering on the tarmac for a welcome ceremony before walking alone to Marine One. He has several events tonight, but he will not leave his hotel for them. A meeting with quad leaders, a U.S.-Thailand trade deal signing and a dinner will all be held at the resort where the president is staying. In other words, he showed up late to this summit, and the summit will be coming to him.

Thousands of protesters have assembled in downtown Manila, chanting in Tagalog, “Trump leave the Philippines.” They are opposed to his Pax Silica A.I. supply chain initiative.

President Trump’s stopover at the Association of Southeast Asian Nations summit is part of a state visit to the Philippines, where he will be received with a 21-gun salute.

The last time he met President Ferdinand R. Marcos Jr. of the Philippines, was full of praise for the Filipino leader.

“Great family, great family legacy and highly respected in his country,” Mr. Trump said while hosting Mr. Marcos at the White House last year.

But that legacy was long considered an obstacle to Mr. Marcos’s political ambitions. He is the son of the dictator Ferdinand E. Marcos, who ruled the Philippines for 20 years, imposed martial law and was ousted in 1986 by a nonviolent uprising. The elder Marcos died in exile in Hawaii, without facing trial on U.S. criminal charges that he had plundered the Philippine Treasury. Years later, a court found his estate liable for torture, summary executions and disappearances.

The younger Marcos spent years rehabilitating his family’s image and won election in 2022. He is hosting Mr. Trump at his official residence, Malacañang Palace, which was also his childhood home.

Mr. Marcos and his wife, Liza Marcos, will receive Mr. Trump on Wednesday with a 21-gun salute. Mr. Trump is expected to sign the palace guest book, hold closed-door meetings, pose for a handshake, then attend a state luncheon held in his honor.

It is not clear whether Mr. Trump, who has a well-known affinity for autocratic leaders, will meet with Mr. Marcos’s mother, Imelda, 97, with whom he has socialized in New York. Ms. Marcos wielded immense power when her husband was president and later came to be known for her flamboyant lifestyle, shoe collection and unexplained wealth.

Mr. Marcos has said that when he called to congratulate Mr. Trump on his election victory in November 2024, Mr. Trump asked about Imelda Marcos.

“My mother is really his friend, he knows my mother very well,” Mr. Marcos said, relaying the conversation. “He was asking about her, ‘How’s Imelda?’ I told him she sends her congratulations.”

After the pomp and circumstance at Malacañang, which traces its roots to the Spanish colonial era in the Philippines, Mr. Trump is headed to Shenzhen, China. The highlight of that leg of his trip will be a trilateral meeting with two strongmen: President Xi Jinping of China and President Vladimir V. Putin of Russia.

For the second year in a row, President Trump is attending the annual gathering of Southeast Asian leaders, a show of alliance to a region that the United States sees as a strategic and maritime counterweight to China.

But Mr. Trump will reach the gathering, held this year in Manila, only on Tuesday, just as the summit wraps up, and he is not expected to make any significant policies or diplomacy moves.

Mixed signals like this — to say nothing of Mr. Trump’s punishing tariffs — are adding to a widespread belief within ASEAN, the Association of Southeast Asian Nations, that the United States is not a reliable partner. That leaves the bloc balancing between Washington and Beijing, with which many of its members have deep trade partnerships.

“They’ve learned that the United States is not reliable, so a lot of the hedging has been away from the United States,” said Ted Osius, a former U.S. ambassador to Vietnam.

The Philippines, America’s oldest security ally in Asia, provides a window into this balancing act.

After taking office three years ago, President Ferdinand R. Marcos Jr. reversed his predecessor’s tilt toward China and pivoted back toward the United States. That stance contributed to many tense encounters between Filipino and Chinese vessels in the South China Sea, where Manila and Beijing have competing territorial claims.

As U.S. patrols in the South China Sea have dropped, Mr. Marcos has appeared to be more conciliatory toward Beijing. In August, he said that there was a “distinct possibility” that the Philippines and China could partner on oil and gas exploration in those waters despite their territorial feud.

“Marcos Jr. already has begun a shift toward China, one that a single visit by Trump is unlikely to stop,” Joshua Kurlantzick, a senior fellow for Southeast Asia and South Asia at the Council on Foreign Relations, wrote last week.

On Sunday, China and ASEAN failed to agree on a code of conduct in the South China Sea — something the Philippines, which has some of the biggest stakes in the dispute, had vowed to secure while chairing the bloc this year.

Chester Cabalza, president of International Development and Security Cooperation, a Philippine think tank, said, “Strategically, Manila opens its doors for conciliation with Beijing to stabilize normal ties. But on the ground, the real battle has yet to subside.”

Domestically, Mr. Marcos has to walk a tightrope, too.

“For Filipino fishermen and coastal communities, the South China Sea is not simply about a geopolitical dispute. It concerns livelihoods, access to maritime resources and national dignity,” said Arnaud Leveau, president of the Asia Centre, a Paris-based think tank. Any diplomatic accommodation perceived as sacrificing Filipino interests, he said, would therefore be politically risky.

The wider region has been battered by the consequences of another Trump policy: the war in Iran. Southeast Asia depends heavily on oil and gas imports from the Middle East and was roiled by soaring prices of fuel. In the Philippines, crops rotted in fields because farmers could not afford to transport them to markets. In Thailand, public servants were asked to use stairs instead of elevators to reduce energy expenses.

ASEAN comprises 11 nations — Brunei, Cambodia, Indonesia, Laos, Malaysia, Myanmar, the Philippines, Singapore, Thailand, Vietnam, and, since last year, East Timor — with vastly different degrees of freedom and wealth. The bloc represents almost 700 million people, or almost 10 percent of the global population.

Making decisions on a consensus-basis and pledging not to interfere in one other’s internal affairs, the bloc has often been criticized as slow and ineffective, with the situation in Myanmar often cited as an example. A coup in that country in 2021 unleashed a civil war that ranks among the world’s most violent conflicts.

Myanmar’s leaders remain barred from ASEAN summits, under an agreement that was extended on Monday. Still, almost half of the bloc’s member states have had bilateral engagements with Myanmar since the coup leader, Min Aung Hlaing, used stage-managed elections to take the post of president this year. Washington has also recently re-engaged in direct talks with Myanmar.

Mr. Trump’s attendance at the summit, which concludes with an ASEAN-U.S. meeting, is part of a state visit to the Philippines. He is expected to hold a bilateral meeting with Mr. Marcos on Wednesday before heading to China for an Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation summit. Mr. Marcos has said little publicly about meeting Mr. Trump; but earlier this year, he described Washington as a “steadfast partner.” His administration has hinted that the two sides are working on a military aid agreement.

In recent days, hundreds of demonstrators have gathered in Manila to protest Mr. Trump’s visit. They oppose his “Pax Silica” initiative, a move to build a supply chain for the artificial intelligence industry that bypasses China, saying it would exploit Philippine natural resources.

Renato Reyes, who led one of the protest groups on Sunday afternoon, said, “Marcos is so desperate to welcome Donald Trump that he will roll out the red carpet, even if Trump arrives late for the summit.”

Aie Balagtas See contributed reporting from Manila and Damien Cave from Ho Chi Minh City, Vietnam.

President Trump sets off this week for meetings in Asia that will culminate in a summit with two strongmen he has often praised: President Xi Jinping of China and President Vladimir V. Putin of Russia.

It will be the first time the three men, who command the world’s largest nuclear arsenals, will have sat down together. Mr. Trump plans to discuss the reduction of nuclear weapons in the world and the war against Iran, according to a White House spokeswoman. He is not expected to bring up Russia’s war with Ukraine.

Mr. Trump will travel first to Manila, then to southern China, and will meet with regional leaders confronting an array of uncertainties. Those include the course of the war between the United States and Iran, high energy prices and mounting concern over the risks of artificial intelligence.

For Mr. Trump, the trip will be a pivot to foreign policy and relationships after American voters delivered a swift rebuke to his party in the midterm elections. His trip also comes as his administration faces scrutiny over a scandal involving the former chief executive of Nvidia, the tech giant, which has raised questions about Chinese espionage and American tech exports.

The highlight of Mr. Trump’s trip is expected to be his meeting with Mr. Xi and Mr. Putin on Thursday in China.

The prospect of their summit has drawn comparisons to the 1945 conference in the Crimean city of Yalta, where the leaders of the United States, the Soviet Union and Britain met to decide the shape of postwar Europe.

The White House has said that Mr. Trump wants to discuss the threat of nuclear proliferation. U.S. officials have expressed concerns about China’s accelerating nuclear buildup, expected to reach 1,000 nuclear warheads by 2030.

The last nuclear arms control treaty between the United States and Russia expired in February. Trump administration officials have said that any new agreement on nuclear arms control must include China, but Beijing has shown no interest in the topic. China has an estimated 620 nuclear warheads, compared with the more than 5,000 that Russia and the United States have each, according to the Stockholm International Peace Research Institute.

U.S. administration officials said they did not expect a deal out of the meeting, which they characterized as a starting point for discussions.

The gathering has prompted concern in Ukraine that Mr. Putin might push for a deal, deciding Ukraine’s fate without Kyiv’s involvement. A U.S. administration official said Ukraine was not expected to be part of the discussion between the three leaders.

Mr. Trump will start his trip in the Philippines, the oldest American treaty ally in the Asia-Pacific region, as the United States faces questions about its support for its partners in a region where China’s military and economic might continues to grow.

In Manila, President Ferdinand R. Marcos Jr. plans to discuss with Mr. Trump the South China Sea, where the Philippines and China have competing claims. Philippine officials have said that they hope Mr. Trump’s visit might result in more American military assistance.

Mr. Trump is also expected to meet with leaders of ASEAN, a Southeast Asian grouping of 11 nations, as well as a gathering of the so-called Quad grouping of the United States, India, Australia and Japan.

Prime Minister Anthony Albanese of Australia said on Sunday that he expected “lots of engagement” with Mr. Trump during the meetings.

“Obviously, that’s our most important ally,” he said. “But we want to see something constructive come out of that, including we want to see a de-escalation in the conflicts that are around the world.”

Chinese analysts described the meeting of the Quad group, formed in part to counter China, as an effort by Mr. Trump to project power after his party’s defeat in the midterms. “Its symbolic value outweighs its practical value,” Xu Liping, a senior scholar at Guangdong University of Foreign Studies, told Shanghai Observer, a state-run outlet.

After Manila, Mr. Trump will travel to the Chinese tech hub of Shenzhen, where he will meet Mr. Xi on the sidelines of the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation.

Jensen Huang, a key adviser who accompanied Mr. Trump on his last trip to China, will not be by his side. Mr. Huang helped shape the president’s views about technological competition with China and persuaded him to loosen restrictions on selling advanced chips to China.

Mr. Huang stepped down from his role at Nvidia after revelations that he had an affair with a Chinese businesswoman, Alice Huang, who U.S. intelligence officials believe was collecting intelligence on Mr. Huang for Beijing. China has rejected the allegations, calling them “smears.” Ms. Huang has not responded to requests for comment.

So far, the president has not raised the issue with Mr. Xi and is not expected to during their meeting on Thursday. But the saga is likely to cause unease in China about whether Washington will impose tighter controls on the export of technology to the country.

As a result, U.S.-based analysts speculated that Mr. Xi might try to use his personal relationship with Mr. Trump to forestall such restrictions.

Yun Sun, director of the China program at the Stimson Center in Washington, said Mr. Huang’s downfall would likely mean “there is going to be more anxiety on the Chinese side about their access to American chips.” She expects Mr. Xi to emphasize the importance of easing U.S. controls on chip exports to China during their meeting.

Cooperation between China and the United States on artificial intelligence may be more difficult after the Nvidia scandal.

Still, there may be progress on the creation of a notification system that would allow the countries to alert each other about national security issues related to artificial intelligence, which the two sides agreed to in September.

“There’s some shared concern on both sides that U.S. or Chinese models might be misused by bad actors and cause damage to the U.S., China, or other countries,” said Kyle Chan, a fellow at the Brookings Institution focusing on China’s technology development.

When Mr. Xi sits down with Mr. Trump in Shenzhen, he will be dealing with a president who will soon face a new constraint at home: a Democrat-controlled Congress.

Lawmakers may pressure Mr. Trump to tighten export controls against China or accelerate weapons sales to Taiwan, according to Ali Wyne, a senior research and advocacy adviser focusing on U.S.-China relations at the International Crisis Group. That means that “Xi will have to work much harder to keep Trump on side,” Mr. Wyne said.

Mr. Trump will be the first American president to visit China twice in one calendar year.

Analysts say the leaders’ summits, which have included state visits in Beijing and then in Washington, have been the only thing holding together a fragile détente between the two countries as they clash over A.I., export controls, Taiwan and other issues.

The two sides declared a trade truce last year when they met in Busan, South Korea. That deal, which was to expire this month, has been extended until January.

China’s restrictions on the export of crucial rare-earth metals and rare-earth magnets, which are needed to manufacture most modern technology, remain one of the most difficult unresolved issues.

Mr. Trump is likely to raise this with Mr. Xi, according to a senior U.S. administration official who said that China’s continued restrictions is one reason Washington only agreed to a two-month extension of the trade truce.

Ana Swanson contributed reporting from Washington, Aie Balagtas See from Manila, and Laura Chung from Sydney, Australia.

China weighs response to US chip curbs before Trump summit

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Beijing vowed “necessary measures” as new US rules target Chinese firms renting Nvidia chips abroad, days before APEC in Shenzhen.

Donald Trump and Xi Jinping at the Great Hall of the People in Beijing, 14 May 2026

China is weighing how hard to hit back at new US limits on its access to AI chips, days before Xi Jinping meets Donald Trump, Bloomberg News reported.

The US Commerce Department released the interim rules on Monday. Overseas data centres now need US approval to give large amounts of computing power to Chinese firms. Shipments of advanced chips to Singapore, Malaysia, Thailand, Indonesia and Brazil also need a licence.

There are exceptions. Chinese firms can still use computing power for content recommendation systems, and they can still rent capacity in US data centres, according to Bloomberg.

On Tuesday, China’s foreign ministry said it would take necessary measures to protect its rights and interests. Spokesperson Guo Jiakun accused the US of abusing export controls.

“It runs counter to the consensus reached between the two sides,” Guo told a regular press briefing in Beijing.

Xi and Trump are due to meet this week on the sidelines of the APEC summit in Shenzhen. It will be their third meeting this year. The two governments are also expected to hold their first dialogue on AI safety.

The rules could strain an unwritten bargain behind the trade truce. Under it, Washington holds off on new tech curbs, and China keeps critical minerals flowing to the US. US officials have complained that rare-earth magnet shipments are arriving too slowly, Bloomberg reported.

Beijing also has a deadline it can use. After Xi and Trump met in Busan last year, China suspended its ban on gallium, germanium and antimony exports to the US. That suspension ends on 27 November, so China could restore the ban simply by letting it lapse.

The curbs target a large source of Chinese computing power. The Institute for Progress, a Washington think tank, had forecast that nearly a third of China’s advanced compute would come from renting overseas data centres by the end of 2027.

Chinese AI stocks fell on Tuesday. Minimax dropped as much as 6.5% and Z.ai fell 6.8%, while chipmaker SMIC rose as much as 9%, Bloomberg reported.

The rules follow the resignation of Jensen Huang, who had been the industry’s loudest critic of chip controls on China. Brian Wong, an assistant professor at the University of Hong Kong, expects a targeted response rather than an end to the truce.

“It puts the tactical pressure on Beijing ahead of the Shenzhen meeting to demonstrate it will not be bullied into submission,” Wong told Bloomberg.
